ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U604	We will move on to agenda item 12: First reading of the draft law amending the Citizenship Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. Please, Minister of the Interior Kristian Jaani!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U605	Good manager! Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I will introduce you to the draft law amending the Citizenship Act developed by the Ministry of the Interior, which was approved by the Government of the Republic at its meeting on 7 April this year and was given to the honourable Riigikogu more than a week later. The draft is short, essentially changes one section of the Citizenship Act. The purpose of the draft is also simple: to give the government the discretion to decide to withdraw Estonian citizenship from those naturalisations who have entered foreign state or military service or intelligence or security services, or to a foreign organisation holding arms and militaryly organized or exercising military exercises. This change allows for example the preservation of Estonian citizenship by naturalisation for those persons who have acquired Estonian citizenship and who are fighting against the Russian Federation in Ukraine. An offence against humanity and an act of aggression shall also be included as the basis for the decision to withdraw citizenship if the person has been convicted of the offences mentioned above. Upon the entry into force of the Act, the Government of the Republic no longer has an impervious obligation to take Estonian citizenship from persons who have entered foreign state or military service or intelligence or security service or armed and militaryly organized or military exercises in a foreign organisation, but the Government of the Republic can consider, for each person, whether or not to be a member of such an organisation may pose a threat to public policy or to the security of the state, and then decide whether the withdrawal of Estonian citizenship is or is not justified. The prior procedure is carried out by the Police and Border Guard Board, which submits a draft order of the Government of the Republic through the Ministry of Internal Affairs to the Government of the Republic for decision. Following the withdrawal of nationality, the person must either apply for a legal basis for his or her stay or leave Estonia. In addition, the current regulation is complemented by a new basis for the withdrawal of Estonian citizenship. Citizenship can be withdrawn by a decision to treat persons convicted of a criminal offence against humanity in the Penal Code under the name "criminal offences against humanity" or for committing an act of aggression. In the case of a conviction of another State, it is necessary to assess whether the conviction of the person has been carried out in accordance with the principles of the State of the law and corresponds to the characteristics of an anti-humanity or aggression crime within the meaning of the Penal Code. Until now, only terrorist offences were the basis for the withdrawal of citizenship. In the case of crimes against humanity, there has also been a course of consideration, not an imperative provision, because in the latter case there is a risk that Estonia will be bound by the judicial system of another country, where the conviction and trial of a person may not take place on the basis of the national principles of law and on the basis of values or democratic rule of law. Citizenship can continue to be taken away from persons who have tried to change the constitutional order of Estonia by violent means, who have concealed circumstances that would have prevented him or her from being granted citizenship or regaining it, or who are in the citizenship of another country but are not exempt from Estonian citizenship, by making false declarations on the acquisition or restoration of Estonian citizenship. Thank you. I'd be happy to answer your questions.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U639	Amendment 13... I'm sorry! The 13th item is the first reading of the draft law supplementing the State Fees Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. Please, social... I think it's the night. Minister of Health and Labour, Tanel Kiik, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U640	Dear Vice-Chairman of the Riigikogu, Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I will brief you on the draft law supplementing the state fee law. The main objective of this proposal for amendment of the law is to support and help health workers from Ukrainian refugees who have reached Estonia so that they can find work faster, more conveniently and at lower cost in Estonia, integrate the Estonian health care system in the best way and start providing the necessary health services to people. The proposal exempts health professionals granted temporary protection from the obligation to pay the state fee both in recognition of their qualifications and when they wish to register themselves as health professionals in different registers. In accordance with the principle of equal treatment, this applies to all beneficiaries of international protection and beneficiaries of temporary protection, i.e. not only to Ukrainian war refugees, but also to other people who on similar grounds, for similar reasons, for example, are forced to leave their home country because of war or other such persecution. This specific change applies in such a way that people have the right to prove their qualification free of state fees or register for a period of one year after obtaining a residence permit on the basis of international protection. Logic here is that, probably at the beginning of the year, people will have more problems economically, more problems even if they are settled, [and should] be supported in the first months, or in the first year, when people are trying to start a new life here, either temporarily or permanently. This change is valid for an indefinite period, which means that it will potentially continue to apply to similar crises in the future. But, as I was referring to, this exemption applies to every person within one year of being granted a residence permit. The costs involved are modest, as we did not take into account the registration of such a greater number of beneficiaries of international protection as a health worker in the planning of the state budget. Of the current beneficiaries of temporary protection, it is estimated that 2% to 3% may be representatives of different health professions. Of course, they are not all going to work and they may not all stay in Estonia at all in the long term. So we do not regard the financial view as significant for this draft. Thank you. I'd be happy to answer the questions.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U647	Good Minister, no further questions. Thank you! By the Social Affairs Committee, Tiiu Aro, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U648	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Colleagues! Minister! The draft presented above was discussed at the Social Affairs Committee meeting on 2 May. Siret Kotka, Hele Everaus, Kert Kingo, Helmen Kütt, Luck Pillak, Priit Sibul, Marika Tuus-Laul, Viktor Vassiliev and Tiiu Aro participated. The Minister of Health and Labour, Tanel Kiik, introduced the bill in the same way that he did in front of us today. Maybe I'm not gonna count it and introduce it to you. A debate followed. Kert Kingo asked why legislative changes are being made on the basis of the current situation rather than resolving the issues raised by a decree of the Government of the Republic. The Minister replied that the amendment of the state fee must, however, change the law. The amendment relates to a narrow target group, which is mentioned by way of exception in the law. Kert Kingo then asked whether those Estonian people who have completed health education abroad still had to pay the state fee. In such a case, there is no equal treatment. Minister Tanel Kiik replied that equal treatment can be given to people in an equal position. In this case, they are people who have left their home country and have to acquire language requirements in the country of destination. If an Estonian citizen has completed an education in Estonia, the state fee is 13 euros, if he has acquired it abroad, it is likely to have been his conscious and free choice. This is not comparable to a situation where a person leaves his home country because of war and is now looking for a professional job. The Minister also noted that the majority of Estonian health professionals have completed their education here in Estonia. Helmen Kütt referred to the explanatory memorandum to the draft, which states that, in order to ensure equal treatment of persons, the provisions of the draft have been extended to all persons who have been granted a residence permit under the Act granting international protection within one year of receiving a residence permit. As a result, he asked whether the change in question would therefore extend to all foreigners who had obtained a residence permit under this law. Tanel swing said yes. That concludes the debate. Procedural decisions followed. First of all, it was proposed that the draft be put on the plenary agenda on 11 May. There was consensus here; all the members of the above-mentioned committee were in favour. Secondly, to appoint a speaker as the representative of the lead committee, and also to be consensual. No one opposed. There were 2: Kert Kingo and Priit Sibul. Thank you!

ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U650	Our 14th item is the draft law amending the Employment Working Conditions Act and Employment Contracts Act initiated by the Government of the Republic of Estonia. Please, Minister of Health and Labour, Tanel Kiik!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U651	Honourable Vice-Chairman of the Riigikogu! Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I am also happy to give an overview of this bill, i.e. the Act amending the Employment Working Conditions Act and the Employment Contracts Act [proposal] 599. This concerns the working conditions of posted workers and is due in particular to the European Commission's letter to Estonia dated 15 July last year on the opening of infringement proceedings. In this case, Estonia is accused of not complying with our national law with Directive 2014/67/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council on the posting of workers in connection with the provision of various services. We need to bring this directive and our national law into line this year and we have prepared the corresponding amendments. The prerequisite for the Directive is that posted workers have the right to claim remuneration from the person who ordered the subcontracting if the employer fails to pay the employee for any reason. The regulation under the Directive was transposed in the case of posted workers, but an equivalent right was not guaranteed for local workers. The European Commission therefore considered that these provisions resulting from the Directive did not apply equally to foreign and domestic service providers in this case which infringe the prohibition of non-discrimination. This is how we give local service providers a competitive advantage. Consequently, a provision laying down the following principles is included in the Employment Contracts Act. A worker working in the construction sector will have the right to claim remuneration not only from his direct employer, but also from the person who ordered subcontracting, i.e. the company that ordered the construction work, i.e. his employer. The employee can claim remuneration if his direct employer fails to fulfil his obligation to pay the salary and the enforcement procedure against him has been unsuccessful for four months, i.e. he has not been able to obtain the promised remuneration within four months, and the employee's claim to the person who ordered the subcontracting, i.e. the person who ordered the work by the employer, is limited to the minimum wage, i.e. no longer to its original basic salary, but to the minimum wage. The person sub-contracting shall not be liable to pay remuneration if he or she has behaved with due diligence of the undertaking. Logic is that if the company that ordered the job is convinced, for example, that its subcontractor is a proper, well-managed institution or company with proper payment behaviour and has no reason to believe that it is unable to pay out these salaries, then in the event of any such problems it will not be obliged to do so either. Logic is that if a shell company or company that clearly has difficulties in payment has been hired or subcontracted knowingly or unintentionally, the person who ordered the subcontracting will be liable to the employees. Similar regulation already applies to posted workers. In addition, some minor technical changes are made to the Working Conditions of Posted Workers Act. For example, some concepts are clarified, the concept of the provision of services is replaced by the narrower term "subcontracting' resulting from the Directive, which is actually one of the points of comment. It also reduces the time limit after which the posted worker can lodge a claim for remuneration against the person who ordered the subcontracting. As I referred to earlier, it will be four months instead of six months to speed up and make it easier for people to get their pay. Thank you. I'd be happy to answer the questions.
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U652	Peter Ernits would like to ask. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U653	Good manager! Good waitress! Another directive has not been transposed, this 2014/67/EU, now in 2022, where we are now. What if we don't take over for some reason? So what happens, what kind of sanctions would we expect?
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U654	Thank you. I have to admit, I can't say the exact penalty rate right now. The logic is that the European Commission will assess the compatibility of national law with the Directive and, if we do not bring it into line, we may be referred to the European Union Court of Justice and the Court may in turn require the necessary measures, which may be both the different basic amounts and the obligations to pay the periodic penalty payments. But these sums would be revealed in the course of the dispute. I think that, given the relatively small impact and technical nature of these amendments, it makes sense for us to make these changes in national law, and thus to close this dispute and the infringement procedure in good time.
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U655	Peter Ernits, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U656	Yes, I have been reading it a lot, but maybe you will be briefing us on what really needs to be done with this directive in order not to get into Brussels's anger and to get a possible punishment. ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U657	In fact, in this case, the draft prepared by the Ministry must be approved. I've already introduced the contents once. We will also make some technical clarifications concerning the period and also the concept of service provision. In this case, we are talking about the construction sector, the construction sector.

ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U663	We have clearly reached our last item today: the first reading of the draft law amending the law on psychiatric assistance and the organisation of health services initiated by the Government of the Republic. Please, Minister of Health and Labour, Tanel Kiik!
ParlaMint-EE_2022-05-11_U664	Honourable Vice-President of Parliament. Happy Members of the Riigikogu! A very beautiful symbolic number, 600 SE, to sum up today. But the subject is important. The amendment of the Psychiatric Assistance Act and the Health Services Organisation Act involves, you can say, two different issues. I'll deal with them in a row. First of all, according to the proposal, we are transferring the funding of psychiatric coercive care from the state budget to the Estonian Health Insurance Fund from 1 July this year and this treatment will be added to the list of funded health care services. Indeed, at the moment, some of the health care services have remained as an historic legacy beyond other sources of funding, but gradually we have transferred them to the health insurance fund in recent years and now psychiatric coercion is in the hands of psychiatric coercion. The Health Insurance Fund already checks all medical bills for psychiatric and essential psychiatric care on the basis of a contract and, for example, pays essential psychiatric assistance to uninsured people, including the provision of care beyond will to health care providers. In order to harmonise this, we will now also include the entire compulsory psychiatric care service provided by the court in the budget of the Health Insurance Fund. The second half of the draft, which we added with the active participation and support of the Social Affairs Committee, deals with various changes in order to improve the availability of mental health care for the people of Estonia. Here too, changes in funding schemes, financing arrangements, as well as legal clarifications, are important. We want to create the necessary foundations so that the health insurance fund can start direct contracts with psychology practitioners to finance psychotherapy. Today, the Health Insurance Fund also finances the relevant service, or psychotherapy, but at the first level it is financed only through the family doctor's therapy fund. This amendment also allows the Health Insurance Fund to conclude direct contracts with psychology practitioners to finance psychotherapy in the future. This will certainly contribute to the availability of mental health services in a context where people's awareness and expectations of them have increased. But also due to both the COVID-19 pandemic and Russia's military activities in Ukraine, there has certainly been a further increase in the need for professional mental health assistance. This is an important step, among others, to improve the availability of these services. With the required volumes, the financing of psychotherapy through the therapeutic fund creates a considerable administrative burden for family doctors. More and more psychology practices tend to favour the growing private market for services financed by the therapeutic fund and those who continue to provide the service often ask patients for so-called "own contribution" to the costs [to cover]. Limited access to services at primary level means increasing pressure on specialist psychiatric care. In fact, it is also unsustainable in the perspective of the financing model, as we see that this treatment gap, the gap in access to treatment between a more prosperous and vulnerable population, is growing. It does not, of course, match the solidarity health system. Of course, we have discussed these changes with people in the field, both with the Estonian Association of Family Doctors and with experts in mental health. In our opinion, the fastest and best possible alternatives in the current situation are to change the legal framework of the health care organisation in the view of both the state and the service providers and patients so that the health insurance fund can start direct contracts with the psychotherapy practices referred to. The legislative changes that are necessary for this purpose. First of all, we bring into law a new legal term to celebrate the main activity of psychology practice, or psychotherapy. We want to include in the Health Services Organisation Act a new type of health care, or psychological treatment, which is psychotherapy and diagnostic support for the treatment of people with mental and behavioural disorders. We also provide for a licence requirement for the provision of psychological treatment in the law. In order for the Health Insurance Fund to be able to conclude contracts with service providers, it is necessary that the relevant authorities have assessed the service providers and are satisfied that they are suitable for the provision of the relevant service. This work is done by the Estonian Health Board. We also exclude clinical psychologists from those involved in the provision of healthcare and include them under the category "person treated as a health worker." This may sound technically, but it is important that this step helps to create the necessary foundations so that companies of clinical psychologists wishing to operate without a doctor can obtain a licence to operate independently and start providing psychological treatment to patients on the basis of a referral from a doctor. Also, and this is at least as important, clinical psychologists have the right to send data directly from the health information system. So far, they have had to use family doctors' treatment summaries, they have sent them encrypted e-mails, which also do not match the modern understanding of e-health. The psychological treatment financed by health insurance is still provided by a doctor's referral, and the decision on mental and behavioural disorders will continue to be made by the doctor, which will not change. The activities of clinical psychologists already active in family practice and hospitals can also continue on current grounds. These amendments are planned to enter into force as a matter of urgency from 1 July, but a number of them require a certain transitional period. For example, the Health Board will start receiving applications for the licences referred to on 1 August this year, and according to the draft, it is proposed that those wishing to provide independent psychological treatment must comply with these new requirements from 1 January 2023. Of course, these changes do not solve all the challenges in the field of mental health, but they are another step towards improving access to these services for people, actually reducing the bureaucratic side, and the flow of data would also be more adequate and rapid, which is critical for every provision of health care. Thank you! I'd be happy to answer the questions.
